I was frightened and a coward, and I was thinking about myself - I could not loose my license as I needed to drive for work and could picture the shame of a court case. I gave very little thought to the person I had hurt. Once home I was agitated and guilty but I had the idea that another drink would be good - If I was traced then I could have said I drank when I retruned home from my sober night - how could they prove differently? </p><p><br></p><p>The next day, driving along the same road, there were yellow triangle signs asking for witnesses. Once home I was agitated and guilty but I had the idea that another drink would be good - If I was traced then I could have said I drank when I retruned home from my sober night - how could they prove differently? </p><p><br></p><p>The next day, driving along the same road, there were yellow triangle signs asking for witnesses. Instead of remorse, I felt relieved that he had not noted my registration and no one else must have come forward. I did cut back my drinking for a period and drive more carefully but to this day, I regret my callousness, I did not even stop to check he was OK. It never made the news so I'm certain he did not die or was permanently injured but who knows. This was 20 years ago, before CCTV and traffic cameras. </p><p><br></p><p>I was morally wrong and it has eaten away at me that I was so selfish. Once home I was agitated and guilty but I had the idea that another drink would be good - If I was traced then I could have said I drank when I retruned home from my sober night - how could they prove differently? </p><p><br></p><p>The next day, driving along the same road, there were yellow triangle signs asking for witnesses. I gave very little thought to the person I had hurt. Once home I was agitated and guilty but I had the idea that another drink would be good - If I was traced then I could have said I drank when I returned home from my sober night - how could they prove differently? </p><p><br></p><p>The next day, driving along the same road, there were yellow triangle signs asking for witnesses. Instead of remorse, I felt relieved that he had not noted my registration and no one else must have come forward. I did cut back my drinking for a period and drive more carefully but to this day, I regret my callousness, I did not even stop to check he was OK. It never made the news so I'm certain he did not die or was permanently injured but who knows. This was 20 years ago, before CCTV and traffic cameras. </p><p><br></p><p>I was morally wrong and it has eaten away at me that I was so selfish. Part of me feels lucky I got away with it and I am a horrible person. I think this may have become a self fullfilling prophesy as i have continued with morally doubtful decisions to this day. I labelled myself as a bad, cowardly person and this is how I carried on. What a shame the accident did not provoke me to be more responsibe, thoughtful and ethical. Once home I was agitated and guilty but I had the idea that another drink would be good - If I was traced then I could have said I drank when I returned home from my sober night - how could they prove differently? </p><p><br></p><p>The next day, driving along the same road, there were yellow triangle signs asking for witnesses. Instead of remorse, I felt relieved that he had not noted my registration and no one else must have come forward. I did cut back my drinking for a period and drive more carefully but to this day, I regret my callousness, I did not even stop to check he was OK. It never made the news so I'm certain he did not die or was permanently injured but who knows. This was 20 years ago, before CCTV and traffic cameras. </p><p><br></p><p>I was morally wrong and it has eaten away at me that I was so selfish. Part of me feels lucky I got away with it and I am a horrible person. I think this may have become a self fullfilling prophesy as i have continued with morally doubtful decisions to this day. I labelled myself as a bad, cowardly person and this is how I carried on. What a shame the accident did not provoke me to be more responsibe, thoughtful and ethical.
